I don't really feel sad or anything like that on a daily basis.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing a lack of energy or feeling fatigued lately, even when you haven't done much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any issues with my energy levels. I don't feel particularly tired or fatigued, just... like I said, I just don't have the drive to do my usual hobbies. But physically, my energy is fine.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about your hobbies and usual activities?\n\nPatient: It's been going on since the scaffold accident... that was during the business readjustment. So, I guess it's been a little over a year now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in how you feel, like trouble concentrating, feeling guilty, or thinking that you're not worth much?\n\nPatient: Well, my concentration is fine, and I don't feel worthless or anything like that. But as for feeling guilty... yeah, I do have that. \n\nThere's just this low-grade dread and guilt that kind of follows me around now. I keep thinking that I should have seen one more risk coming with that scaffold, you know? People keep telling me that all the proper controls were in place, but... I still just feel like I should have caught it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things even when others have reassured you that you're not at fault?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly what I mean. It's really just tied to that scaffold incident. Even with everyone telling me I did everything right and it wasn't my fault, I still blame myself for it. It's pretty much just focused on that one specific event.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that this guilt about the scaffold incident affects how you see yourself overall, or do you feel it’s limited to thoughts about that specific event?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say it affects how I see myself overall. It's just... that one day, that one event where I feel like I missed something. But outside of that, my overall view of myself hasn't really changed.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often this lack of interest in your usual activities occurs? Is it every day, most days, or more occasional?\n\nPatient: It's most days. Honestly, that's what finally made me agree to come in and talk to someone. I just... I don't care about the things that used to keep me balanced. \n\nThings like sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, or even just tinkering out in the woodshop. It's just that one specific event that I can't seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Outside of the feelings related to the scaffold incident, do you ever feel a general sense of emptiness or sadness that lasts for most of the day, even when there’s no specific reason for it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I wouldn't say I feel empty or sad most of the day. Like I said, I just don't have the energy or interest for my usual hobbies right now, but it's not really a general sadness.
